# READING TEST

In the Reading test, you will read a variety of texts and answer several different types of reading comprehension questions. The entire Reading test will last 75 minutes. There are three parts, and directions are given for each part. You are encouraged to answer as many questions as possible within the time allowed.

You must mark your answers on the separate answer sheet. Do not write your answers in your test book.

### Questions 149–150 refer to the following text message chain.

**Arnold Durant [8:47 A.M.]**
Brandon, are you on your way to the office?

**Brandon Fox [8:49 A.M.]**
I just got in. Is something wrong?

**Arnold Durant [8:50 A.M.]**
There was an accident on the highway, and I'm stuck in traffic. I don't think I'll have time to clean up our conference room before the client meeting at 9:30. Are you busy this morning?

**Brandon Fox [8:51 A.M.]**
Not at all. How many people are you expecting?

**Arnold Durant [8:53 A.M.]**
Six altogether. I owe you one. I'll take you out to lunch sometime this week.

### Questions 151–152 refer to the following information.

#### Holiday Bus Timetable

Buses on Route 181 will operate on an amended schedule during the holiday season to accommodate public and private events. The route begins at Bobcat Stadium, running through the downtown area and terminating at Grenada Plaza. The last bus usually departs the stadium at 10:00 P.M. on weekdays and 10:30 P.M. on weekends. From December 29 to January 3, it will run later than usual, with the last bus departing at 11:00 P.M. on December 29–31 and at 11:45 P.M. on January 1–3.

### Questions 153–155 refer to the following memo.

From: Tonya Keiper
To: Sales representatives
Date: July 14
Subject: Trek

The Lorimor R&D team has finalized the latest version of our GPS navigation system, Lorimor Trek 2.0. There are several differences between this model and the previous one. We've added a voice-activated command center, which will allow drivers to perform operations without using the touchscreen. We've also made the dashboard unit slightly larger, and there is a special coating on the screen to reduce glare.

We were able to use a lot of ideas from after-purchase feedback shared by our customers who are using the original Lorimor Trek. We already have a detailed user manual ready for the system, but next week I'm going to try to make it shorter so that users can read through it quickly and start using the Lorimor Trek 2.0 right away. Please be prepared to explain the differences to customers.

### Questions 168–171 refer to the following article.

RICHMOND (August 16)—Valley Hardware Store has announced that it will move to the Hillside neighborhood next month. The hardware store's new home will be in the former Orton Department Store building on Ramsey Avenue. The store's owner, Yolanda Joyce, hopes that the new site will better serve customers. "People are trying do-it-yourself projects at home more frequently these days instead of relying on tradespeople who do those kinds of projects for a living. — [1] —. So, they need the right materials to get the job done, and we need more space to provide what they need," Joyce said.

— [2] —. The business's employees will regularly show shoppers how to carry out simple projects such as varnishing furniture, removing paint, and installing curtain rods. "We want to give our customers the support and confidence they need to get the job done," said general manager William Clifton.

— [3] —. Another change on the horizon is an intensive training program for employees. "Through our most recent market research, we learned that customers buy more when they are assisted by staff members who are well-acquainted with the products and their uses," added Clifton. — [4] —.

### Questions 172–175 refer to the following article.

#### The Sweet Taste of Success

STREAMWOOD (9 November)—Located on a small street in a quiet town, a little bakery has begun to get noisy. Gilmore's Cakes has become so famous in such a short time that people are requesting cakes for their events up to as much as a year in advance.

One thing that sets this bakery apart from others is that every cake is different. Depending on the occasion, owner and head baker Samuel Gilmore changes something to make it fit the event. It could be the color, the decorations, or even individual taste preferences.

Mr. Gilmore says that he got the idea for his business from his family. "When I was growing up, my grandma would always make a special birthday cake for family members on their birthdays," he explains. "After attending culinary school, I wanted to create a personal touch that customers would be able to enjoy."

When Gilmore's Cakes opened three years ago, it did not enjoy the kind of attention that it does today. Some locals enjoyed its products, but special orders were few and far between. After unsuccessful attempts of advertising in newspapers and on local radio, Mr. Gilmore decided to try a more modern technique by offering special deals to customers who posted about his business on social media. Within weeks, images of cakes made by Mr. Gilmore were all over the Internet and the demand for his services have skyrocketed.

The American Culinary Committee (ACC) has invited Mr. Gilmore to its headquarters in Bakersfield to be officially recognized as this year's National Cake Master. The award ceremony will be attended by celebrity chef Mila Baldwin and broadcasted live on Edibles Network.

172. The word "noisy" in paragraph 1, line 2 is closest in meaning to
     (A) unpleasant
     (B) delicious
     (C) famous
     (D) festive

173. According to the article, what is special about goods made by Gilmore's Cakes?
     (A) They are made with all organic ingredients.
     (B) They have received awards in local cooking contests.
     (C) They are shaped like famous celebrities.
     (D) They feature personalized designs and flavors.

174. How did Mr. Gilmore most effectively promote his business?
     (A) By creating SNS promotions
     (B) By posting billboards
     (C) By writing to the local paper
     (D) By airing radio ads

175. What will Mr. Gilmore probably do in Bakersfield?
     (A) Join a committee
     (B) Receive an award
     (C) Host a television show
     (D) Judge a competition

### Questions 176–180 refer to the following interview transcript and schedule.

> **Community Spotlight on Ashley Gilbert**
> Interviewed by Earl Schultz
>
> Excerpt below from May 3 interview with Ashley Gilbert, who is offering a new class in Mountainview.
>
> **Earl Schultz:** Cooking has always been a popular hobby. How does your class tie in with that?
>
> **Ashley Gilbert:** Well, in the digital age, people are looking for ways to share their creations on social media, blogs, and more. I want to help people capture their food presentation in a professional way.
>
> **ES:** I love cooking, and I'm interested in photography, too. How can your class help a complete beginner like me, who doesn't know anything about taking photos of food as an art form but wants to learn how to do it better? Would I have to buy a high-end camera to participate in the class?
>
> **AG:** Not at all. In the very early days of my photography business, I was getting by with only an inexpensive used camera I bought from a Web site.
>
> **ES:** That's reassuring for people who aren't sure whether they want to fully commit to this hobby.
>
> **AG:** Exactly. Food photography is less about the device you're using and more about how you present the subject matter in the composition and framing. Lighting also makes a big difference. In fact, that's why I'm so pleased my class will be held in Braxton Hall, which has a lot of natural light as well as a modern lighting system.

#### Mountainview Community Events: Classes in June

**Dessert Creations**

- **Date & Time:** June 9, 2 P.M.–4 P.M.
- **Location:** Greenway Plaza
- **Details:** Learn decorating techniques for beautiful cakes and other desserts that are worthy of a blog post. [Fee: $35]

**Growing Herbs**

### Questions 165-167 refer to the following article.

**VT Lighting Completes Acquisition Deal**

BERLIN (December 4)—VT Lighting, a popular producer of lighting equipment in Europe, announced yesterday that it has completed a deal to acquire U.S.-based Swoboda Inc. The acquisition is the first step in the company's expansion into North America. Swoboda has one of the country's largest networks of storage facilities and transportation systems, which will be invaluable for distributing VT's goods throughout North America. Swoboda also produces a line of its own lighting for offices, hospitals, retail enterprises, and more, nicely complementing VT's line of residential lighting.

"We want to expand to other areas, but it's important for us to make sure we are on top of the logistics. That's why we're pleased to acquire Swoboda, as it is locally based and its employees know the regulations better," said Daniel Murray, a spokesperson for VT Lighting.

This deal marks the first acquisition by VT Lighting, but it certainly won't be the last. The company is already looking into making a similar arrangement when it expands into South America next year. With steady growth, VT is well positioned to become a worldwide market leader.

### Questions 168-171 refer to the following e-mail.

**To:** All Starcast Travel Agency Employees  
**From:** Michelle Petrillo  
**Date:** March 3  
**Subject:** Industry conference

Dear Employees,

For the first time in its history, the National Travel Conference (NTC) will be held right here in Pittsburgh from May 3 to May 7. I hope that you will all take advantage of this excellent opportunity to learn more about our industry and network with other professionals. It has just been announced that the keynote speaker will be Anthony Shaw, host of the popular daytime television program _Passport to Paradise_. Other notable speakers include Junpei Kouki, who has written more than 20 guidebooks based on his international travels, and Denise Egan, who has been blogging and traveling across the globe for the past five years. A complete list of talks, booths, and workshops is available on the NTC Web site at www.nationaltravel.org/schedule.

I would like you all to attend as many sessions as possible, but we still need to keep the office open. Therefore, please e-mail me sometime this week about your top choices for activities. Then I'll create a schedule that will accommodate everyone's desired choices as best as possible. For particularly popular activities, I will use seniority to determine who will go to the conference and who will stay behind. However, for those of you who don't get your first choice, I'll do my best to get you your second or third choice.

Sincerely,

Michelle Petrillo

### Questions 176-180 refer to the following article and e-mail.

**Springfield Tribune**

SPRINGFIELD (Monday, May 14)—If you were to view the Velex Building from above, you'd see that it is bursting with vegetation, color, and energy. The building's owner, Jacob Feldman, has just completed a project to add a rooftop garden to the building, thanks to the Community Enrichment Grant, which is awarded annually by the Harper Foundation. The grant has been awarded for Springfield-based projects twice in the past, once for renovation work at St. Mary Chapel, which dates back to the 1700s, and once for repairs at the former City Hall building, which was used by city employees over 150 years ago.

Mr. Feldman applied for the grant last year, but it was the changes to the design and the addition of a public access component that finally gave him a successful grant proposal this year. The rooftop features benches and a small picnic area for Springfield residents, surrounded by planting beds full of beautiful flowers and fragrant herbs. A collection of photos of the garden, and additional information, will be printed in next week's edition of _Week Around Town_.

---

**To:** Jacob Feldman <jacobfeldman@feldmanproperties.com>  
**From:** Edith Landers <e.landers@happycafe.net>  
**Date:** Tuesday, May 23  
**Subject:** Rooftop garden

Dear Mr. Feldman,

I saw photos of your building's rooftop garden in the local magazine that came out today. I was delighted to learn about your project and to see that you are growing edible plants such as herbs. I run the Happy Café downtown as well as a blog called _Living Together_, and I am wondering about what you plan to do with the herbs from your rooftop. Would you be interested in selling some of them to my café? I try to use only locally supplied ingredients in my sandwiches, which were featured in the _Local Eats_ guidebook. I used to grow herbs on my café's own rooftop, but I had to get rid of the planting beds to add more tables and chairs for my customers. It was a difficult choice, but I simply did not have enough room for people dining in, and moving wasn't an option because other buildings in my area were out of my price range. Anyway, if you would be interested in supplying herbs for my café, please e-mail me back or call me at 555-4861.

I hope to hear from you soon!

Edith Landers

1. Tour Guide, Santa Cruz Bird Sanctuary, Miraloma

Due to safety concerns, we require a letter of reference that speaks to your ability to follow instructions carefully.

**Rowan Wheels**

Austin's most popular supermarket, Rowan Supermarket, is pleased to launch its new food-delivery service—Rowan Wheels. Customers may shop for groceries online and have them sent to the address of their choice at the touch of a button. The items will be collected from the Rowan Supermarket branch closest to the delivery address.

Paradise Lumber has recently announced its intention to launch its first-ever lumber shop. The site, which will feature 14 types of lumber, will sell this wood and wood-related tools to individuals. The shop will be located in the popular suburb of Waverly Hills. Its target demographic will be homeowners working on do-it-yourself projects. ### Questions 161-163 refer to the following article.

**Keeping Finances Fit**

LITTLETON (August 14)—Many fitness facilities have multiple classrooms for group activities. However, as classes are not scheduled around the clock, these rooms often sit empty for a large proportion of the day.

Some gym owners are looking for ways to make these spaces useable so that they can generate income even when fitness classes are not being conducted. Theo Tremblay, the owner of Iron Gym, has recently launched a new program called Assembly-360. Through the program, groups in the area—such as community clubs and small businesses—can rent the unused classrooms on an hourly basis for meetings and training events.

"There is a significant lack of public meeting spaces in Littleton, so people often have to book far in advance just to get a spot. With Assembly-360, they have more options available," said Tremblay.

So far, Iron Gym already has had over twenty bookings through the program. Tremblay has invested in tables and chairs that can be set up and taken down quickly, converting the room from a workout space to a meeting room. In addition, the padded walls provide excellent soundproofing so that meeting attendees are not disturbed by the other gym sounds. To further improve the experience of meeting participants, Tremblay provides hot coffee and tea along with some popular snacks as part of the service.

"I was worried about the atmosphere of this space," said Marsh Wilson, who held a meeting at Iron Gym last week, "but I was pleasantly surprised by how the room was transformed."

161. What is suggested about Mr. Tremblay?  
     (A) He holds meetings with his staff regularly.  
     (B) He is concerned about the competition in the area.  
     (C) His business has more than one room for classes.  
     (D) His gym offers classes for all levels.

     (B) Presentation materials  
     (C) Refreshments  
     (D) Parking

To: All Florene Consulting Employees  
From: Neil Wakefield, Senior Strategist  
Date: February 15  
Re: Info

Ever since Ms. Benoit announced her retirement plans several months ago, we have been searching far and wide for a suitable replacement. As the recruitment firm we usually use went out of business, we posted the position on numerous external Web sites. Jean Stokes in the HR department put in a lot of hours researching the sites that would give us the type of candidate we were looking for.

In the end, we opted to fill Ms. Benoit's position from our existing staff by promoting Ella Massey. She will take over Ms. Benoit's duties starting from March 1, and she is prepared for the many domestic and overseas business trips that are required in that department.

Also on March 1, Gerald Auger will join our team to take over Ms. Massey's position. He has five years of experience at Hillcrest Advisors. His knowledge of the industry, as well as his strong people skills, really stood out in last month's group interview, and we were even more impressed when we got the chance to speak to him one-on-one earlier this month. We're looking forward to continuing to provide an excellent level of customer care with these two personnel changes.

### Questions 172-175 refer to the following article.

**Hightower Chapel Faces Uncertain Future**

STOCKTON (May 8)—Five years ago, Hightower Chapel held its last service and then went on the market. The historical building, dating back to the 1800s, is in need of extensive repairs. After the chapel was on the market for two years, the city of Stockton purchased the building and assumed responsibility for the restoration.

Initially, locals were in favor of the purchase, but the restoration project has not moved forward as promised. "I fully support saving old buildings, but the city needs to take action. The houses in this neighborhood are well-cared for by their owners, and this is a stark contrast to the chapel," said Pablo Cavillo, who lives near the chapel.

"I don't understand why construction hasn't begun yet. I'm tired of seeing this eyesore every day. Someone needs to fix this mess," added Kelly Garner.

Mayor Rhoda Moore was reluctant to purchase the building at first, but he was persuaded to go ahead with the project after a city-employed financial consultant convinced him that it would be good for tourism. Moore plans to hold a meeting next month to discuss the chapel and hear opinions from the public.

### Questions 176-180 refer to the following Web page and e-mail.

**Toronto Museum of Natural History**

**Photography Policy**  
Visitors to the Toronto Museum of Natural History are welcome to take photographs of the items on display if the flash on their camera or phone is turned off. Please note that these photos must be for personal use only. They cannot be sold or printed on items for sale.

**Artifacts Room**  
The Artifacts Room is used for restoring items from our collection. Visitors must be university faculty or students. When applying for a pass, please indicate the institution where you work/study and the reason you want to visit the room. In addition, visitors must be a museum member or be accompanied by a museum member, and the membership number should be provided for our records.

You can make a request by completing a request form and e-mailing it to artifacts@tmnh.ca, and we will call you back to discuss the project further and arrange a visiting time.

To: artifacts@tmnh.ca  
From: g_owens@trc1.com  
Date: November 3  
Subject: Artifacts room  
Attachments: request_form.docx

To Whom It May Concern:

My name is Gary Owens, and I'm a graduate student in film studies at the University of Toronto. I would very much like to film some of your employees at work in the Artifact Room, and I've attached the completed request form that you require.

I am currently working on a documentary about the artifact recovery process. The film received a federal grant to help with the costs, and it is aimed at educating the public about archeology. I plan to release the film online sometime next summer, and people will be able to watch it for free.

My schedule is very flexible, so I don't think there will be any trouble finding a time to visit that will work well for your team.

I look forward to hearing from you,

Gary Owens

### Questions 181-185 refer to the following article and e-mail.

**Price Jump at Racine Semiconductors**

January 3—Racine Semiconductors has announced plans to increase the sale price of its goods. The increase will affect both its silicon-based products and those containing other materials. The change will result in price increases of 3 to 7 percent, which may, in turn, drive up the price of consumer goods that contain electronic circuits.

Racine Semiconductors has three plants throughout Arizona—its main plant in Tucson, and two smaller plants in Mesa and Glendale. Though profits have been steady over the past few years, the company has been cited on several safety violations. The price changes will help to create enough of a surplus to invest in better protective gear as well as increase the amount of training in an effort to decrease workplace injuries.

"We strive to create the best product while also taking care of our workers. This price change will allow us to do that," said Debbie Jorgensen, a company spokesperson.

To: Sunder Patel <s.patel@ellisonelectronics.com>  
From: Glenda Zayas <glendazayas@racine.net>  
Date: January 7  
Subject: RE: Ellison monthly order  
Attachment: rate_card.docx

Dear Mr. Patel,

I received your message regarding the new price changes at Racine. For existing clients, we thought it would be fitting for us to keep the old rates for sixty days so that companies have time to adjust to the price difference. Therefore, both your January and February orders will not be affected at all. I have attached a copy of the new rate card, which will be useful information to you as head of the purchasing department.

Ellison Electronics is currently classified as a medium-volume client. If you were able to increase your monthly unit orders by just five percent, it would put you in the category of high-volume client. In that position, you would be eligible for a discount that would cancel out the price increase. I understand that you plan to visit our main plant next week, so we can discuss it then. Or you can reach me in the meantime by calling 250-555-4431, extension 12.

Glenda

Dear Employees,

As you know, the entire Blane Industries staff will move to the Dalton Building temporarily while extensive renovations are being carried out on our own building. Renovations will begin on May 15, and we expect them to take approximately eight months. The day before the work begins, employees should get boxes and tape from the HR office in order to pack up their personal items. Be sure that your name and floor number in the Dalton Building are clearly labeled on the side of any box you pack. This will make it easier for the movers to take the items to the right place.

Victor Finley

March 8

Dear Ms. Turner,

Centennial Supplies is the area's leading supplier of hotel housekeeping needs. Our range of sheets, pillowcases, blankets, and mattress protectors provide luxury and comfort to your guests, and they're easy to clean.

Mingara Hotel could surely benefit from using our products. According to a recent industry-wide survey, customers prefer our products to those of our competitors at a rate of two to one. One of our sales representatives would be happy to bring over some samples for you to view, and I can also send you a product catalog. Simply reply to this e-mail address for more information.

Warmest regards,

Savannah Galway  
Business Development Manager, Centennial Supplies

To: Savannah Galway  
From: Phillip Santiago  
Date: March 15  
Subject: Pillowcases

Dear Ms. Galway,

I appreciate your understanding regarding the issue with my department. This is the first shipping error we've had in months, but it affected everyone in the Somerton delivery area. Unfortunately, some of the boxes in the warehouse were mislabeled, so my team sent the wrong sizes to several corporate customers.

Replacements have already been sent, but I think it would be best if you could also call the affected customers to personally apologize. I would do it myself, but the customers already know you. During that call, please also ask customers whether they would like a $25 voucher for a future purchase or an additional free product with a value of $25. For anyone who chooses the product, just let me know, and I will send it by express mail.

I have new procedures in place to help avoid this mislabeling problem in the future.

Phillip Santiago

### Questions 161–163 refer to the following article.

#### Start-Up Business Seeks to Reduce Effect of Fast Fashion

By Stephen Hampton

BRANCHBURG (January 8)—With the new year comes a new idea for helping the environment. The start-up company C-Loop is buying and selling used clothing in Branchburg. Janet Roscoe got the idea for C-Loop after watching a documentary on fast fashion. "These days, people can buy clothing cheaply, wear it for a short time, and then throw it away. This is very harmful to the environment," Roscoe said.

C-Loop, which stands from "Clothing Loop", is a way for residents of Branchburg to get the most out of their clothing. Janet Roscoe can download the C-Loop smartphone app to view the items of clothing that are currently available. With just a few taps, their order will be sent to their home, as long as it is within the Branchburg city limits. They can also sell items and receive credit in their account.

In addition to saving clothing from heading straight to the landfill, C-Loop also reduces the amount of new clothing that needs to be sold in town. This is another way to help the environment, as there will be fewer trucks transporting goods the long travel distance from neighboring towns.

You can sign up for a C-Loop account with just a five-dollar deposit by visiting www.c-loop.com.

### Questions 172–175 refer to the following article.

#### KTPR Radio Announces New Host

October 14—KTPR Radio has announced that Austin Townsend will be stepping down from his position as the long-time host of _Feel the Beat_. The program, a staple of the station's programming, has been around for three decades, with Mr. Townsend hosting his first show five years ago. _Feel the Beat_ is broadcast every Tuesday and Friday evening, and it features music from a wide range of genres.

In a radio segment yesterday, a spokesperson for KTPR shared plans to replace Mr. Townsend with Marvin Spencer, a former music critic for the _Daily Herald_. In two months, Mr. Townsend will host his final show, which will include live performances by local bands. Then the show will take a break to make room for holiday programming. From the first of the year, guest hosts will cover the duties until Mr. Spencer takes on his role on February 1.

Mr. Spencer knows the music scene in and out, and he is looking forward to bringing some fresh music to the radio station. "Mainstream musical groups can be heard on any station," Mr. Spencer said during the radio announcement yesterday. "I've always challenged my audience to expand their horizons and give new music a chance. In past projects, I made an effort to bring undiscovered yet talented musicians to the forefront. _Feel the Beat_ will allow me to do this on an even larger scale."

When the Warrensburg's Parks and Recreation budget was slashed due to budget shortfalls, the nonprofit sector stepped in to provide funding for garden designs in parks throughout the town. Dear Staff,

I am writing to you all to encourage you to share your best wishes with Nicole Lauer at a luncheon on July 10. Ms. Lauer was recently presented with this year's Industry Innovator Award. This national award is given to a person working with renewable energy who has contributed to the field in a major way. In addition to this being a prestigious award, it is also the first time in twenty-five years that it has been won by someone from a company as young as ours. The attention gained from Ms. Lauer's work is particularly well timed, as it assisted in our company's negotiations to be bought out by Rockport Energy.

The luncheon will be held at Bellevue Plaza and will begin at 11:30 A.M. Those of you working in the Albany Building will be able to walk there on foot, while the rest of the staff will need to arrange carpools. We need a final head count for the meal no later than July 2, so please e-mail Maritza Perez, in the Fairfax Building, to let her know whether or not you plan to be in attendance.

Sincerely,  
Ralph Hamilton

# READING TEST

In the Reading test, you will read a variety of texts and answer several different types of reading comprehension questions. The entire Reading test will last 75 minutes. There are three parts, and directions are given for each part. You are encouraged to answer as many questions as possible within the time allowed.

You must mark your answers on the separate answer sheet. Do not write your answers in your test book.

### Questions 151-152 refer to the following article.

**Petunia Accessories Goes Vegan**

Petunia Accessories—a brand known for its fashionable handbags, hats, and belts—is branching out into new territory. The company has recently signed a contract with EcoLove to use its fabric in the production of a line of handbags. EcoLove manufactures vegan-friendly fabric that looks and feels like leather but does not contain any animal products. Through the agreement, the fabric will be the first-ever component in Petunia's products that was not produced by one of the Petunia plants. Petunia officials hope that introducing the new line will show the company's dedication to responding to its customers' needs.

### Questions 153-154 refer to the following article.

The Toronto Museum of Art (TMA) has delayed the opening of the "Hands and Hearts" painting exhibit, which was originally set to debut on May 17. The newly scheduled date is May 24. A TMA spokesperson blamed the replacement of the exhibition hall's flooring, which was behind schedule due to a delivery issue, as the grounds for the change.

The exhibit will feature paintings that are on loan from the Modern Art Center in Vancouver. The opening will include a talk from Lynda Doran, who is prominently featured in the collection. Tickets for the May 24 event can be purchased online for $25 each, and all proceeds will go toward museum projects. The regular admission fee of $5 will apply to the remainder of the exhibition, which will run for two weeks.

**Eckart, Bonnie [9:43 A.M.]**  
Hi, everyone. Since some of you need to be off-site this afternoon, I've moved the team meeting to tomorrow. However, I wanted to see if there were any pressing issues. How is the Ocala project coming along? Can we still have the painters begin on July 12?

**Sierra, Michael [9:44 A.M.]**  
We're behind schedule on the hotel lobby because the light fixtures we're importing from Italy were held up at customs upon arrival in England due to some missing paperwork.

**Lassiter, Caroline [9:46 A.M.]**  
We had a similar problem with customs on the project for Glimmer Jewelry, so that's something to look into. In this case, the client has also asked for so many changes from our original design plan. It seems that even perfection is not good enough for them.

**Sierra, Michael [9:47 A.M.]**  
That's right! It's more than I anticipated.

**Eckart, Bonnie [9:48 A.M.]**  
Well, we've already accepted the contract, so there's no going back now. Should I reschedule the painters?

**Dwyer, Regina [9:50 A.M.]**  
We might have everything soon, in which case there would be no need to change the schedule. See you all at tomorrow's meeting.

**Eckart, Bonnie [9:51 A.M.]**  
OK! And now that we've covered all that, let's decide who is handling which jobs for the second phase of the project then.

Nicholas Holmes  
371 Gaudet Avenue  
Arlington, TX 76019

September 23

Dear Mr. Holmes,

Yesterday, September 22, I received your letter regarding your visit to our restaurant, The Wheelhouse, on September 20. — [1] —. I apologize that our staff did not seat you as soon as you arrived despite your having a reservation, and that you were frustrated by the long wait. According to our reservation policy, if guests do not arrive within fifteen minutes of their allotted reservation, the table is given to someone else. — [2] —. I understand that you had to drive around for a long time to find parking, which caused the problem. Unfortunately, the lack of parking for customers is an ongoing issue for us.

We hope that you will give our business another chance. With this in mind, I have enclosed a voucher for $10, which is valid at The Wheelhouse as well as The Ember Grill. — [3] —. The Ember Grill is within walking distance to a subway station and a bus stop so that may be more convenient for you. It also has stunning city views, as it's on the top floor of the Penwell Building, and the menu is the same as that of The Wheelhouse. — [4] —.

Warmest regards,

Veronica McCrory  
General Manager, The Wheelhouse and The Ember Grill

**8th Annual Plastics in Pittsburgh Expo (PPE)**  
**November 3–4 at Waldeck Exhibition Center, Pittsburgh, USA**

PPE is the largest exposition of plastic producers in North America. Whether you're looking to get your product on the market or just shopping around for the most technologically advanced plastics, we've got you covered.

Early sign-up for vendors (before September 1) results in priority booth placement as well as a substantial discount. Complete the registration form on the For Vendors tab above. Online registration for vendors continues until October 20. We have recently changed our rules for visitors. Attendance is now open to everyone, rather than just those working directly in the industry. Tickets for visitors can be ordered through our Web site, by e-mail, or by mail until October 25. After that date, only in-person purchases at the entrance will be available.

Vendors can begin setting up their product displays on November 2. Please note that both vendors and visitors are prohibited from videotaping the proceedings or producing audio copies.

Sincerely,

Min Huang, Beisha Manufacturing

**Whitaker Inc.**  
Standard Procedures (last update: August 12)

**Equipment**

- **Equipment Tracking:** When using equipment for any of the on-site experiments you are performing, be sure to indicate the sign-out date in the equipment-tracking book, along with the estimated return date.
- **Sterilization:** All equipment should be sterilized after use to avoid cross-contamination. Please use the Steri-10 machine whenever possible, as it consumes very little electricity. The Roth-360 machine should only be used for very large items.

**Building**

- Outside food or drink is not permitted in the building at any time, with the exception of the break room, located in the north wing.
- The east wing of the building is a climate-controlled environment, as the work performed there must be carried out at a very specific temperature and humidity. The entrance has two sets of doors to create an airlock in between. During certain periods of the day, entry may be prohibited, as indicated by the red warning light.
- If you are the last person to leave your section, be sure to use your employee code to set the security alarm and make sure the security camera is on (you will hear a whirring sound).

**To:** Miranda Jasper  
**From:** Daksh Nandi  
**Date:** August 13  
**Subject:** Standard Procedures Summary

Dear Miranda,

I wanted to thank you for taking care of revising the standard operating procedures at the last minute to be ready before the new hires arrived. I think everyone will find them useful. There is one change that I would like to propose, however. It would be best to delete the section about inputting employee codes. Those tasks are no longer relevant because we've outsourced that work to Upton Enterprises.

I will hang up some additional notices around the building to help employees remember specific procedures. Keep up the great work!

Sincerely,

Daksh Nandi

**ATTENTION!**  
Do not proceed into this area if the red light is on.  
We appreciate your compliance.  
Whitaker Inc.

### Questions 196-200 refer to the following article, advertisement, and e-mail.

RIO DE JANEIRO (January 27)—Primor Foods has confirmed that after two years of in-depth research, it is finally ready to release its newest product, Soy-Press. This soybean plant produces an oil that has the highest known smoke point among edible oils, meaning it can be used for frying at very high temperatures.

Soy-Press is grown in the southeast region of Brazil. It has a wide range of cooking applications, and consumers can find it in supermarkets from next month.

Development of an enhanced version of the Soy-Press plant will get underway next week, as Primor Foods begins recruiting staff to create a plant with a growth cycle of just 30 days compared to other soy plants' current rate of about 50 days.

Join the Primor Foods Team in our Research & Development, Public Relations, and Purchasing departments!

We will work to develop SP-Max, the enhanced version of our company's popular Soy-Press plant.

A letter of recommendation from the applicant's most recent supervisor is required for all positions. Research & Development employees must have a bachelor's degree or higher in a related scientific field and provide proof of such. Those applying for Public Relations positions must submit a sample press release. At least one year of experience is required, but preference will be given to those with three years or more. Top-performing employees are eligible for quarterly bonuses.

To find out more about job opportunities at Primor Foods, visit www.primorfoods.br.

**To:** i_almeida@salvadoruniversity.edu  
**From:** danilosantos@velozenviar.br  
**Date:** August 8  
**Subject:** Thank you!

Dear Ms. Almeida,

Thank you for sending a copy of my degree and my official transcripts so quickly so that I could submit the required materials for my job application at Primor Foods. I've been offered the position I wanted, and I just signed the employment contract this morning. I appreciate your help.

Sincerely,

Danilo Santos

> ## Grande Villarreal

> Grande Villarreal is opening its 50th location this summer, and this one will be near Islip Airport. The new location plans to open on June 6, and is currently recruiting receptionists, housekeepers, and kitchen staff. Since it is Grande Villarreal's policy to recruit management positions from within, all of them will be filled by transferring employees from other locations. Candidates can apply for positions on Grande Villarreal's Web site at www.grandevillarreal.net/hiring. Interviews will be conducted throughout the month of May, and the positions are planned to be filled by June 1 in order to allow a few days for training. **Questions 164–167 refer to the following article.**

> ## Ashburn Café Anniversary Celebration
>
> (12 May)—Next Monday, 19 May, marks the ten-year anniversary of Ashburn Café. Mark Hamlin of the _Georgia Times_ interviewed the owner of Ashburn Café, Donna Combs, about the café and its continuous prosperity.
>
> **MH:** For the past decade, Ashburn Café has been a landmark for tourists and locals alike. What is the secret to your business' success?
>
> **DC:** Well, we have always done our best to provide quality baked foods and beverages at reasonable prices. Of course, every café does that, though. I think that we were really able to stand out from the others by offering free items to customers who made positive posts about our café on social media. Social media marketing wasn't nearly as big a thing back then, but it really took off.
>
> **MH:** You mentioned that you sell baked foods and beverages. As a long-time regular customer, I see that the menu changes regularly. How do you decide what to keep and what to add?
>
> **DC:** Our menu also gets a lot of influence from social media. The items that are most commonly posted about are kept, and we always listen to suggestions and requests from our customers. Our bakers and baristas are always coming up with new ideas based on our customers' input. That's how we come up with new additions. Some stay for years, and some get replaced before long.

> **FOR IMMEDIATE RELEASE**
>
> Rivard Confirms New CEO
>
> MELBOURNE (3 March)—Rivard, the nation's leading financial consulting firm, has announced that Katharine Martz has been selected as the company's new CEO. Graduating from Australian National University (ANU), Ms. Martz was immediately accepted into an internship program at Hubbard Co. While there, she created an online course for financial advisors that is still used today. She worked her way up to a management position and was then recruited by Landry Financial to oversee their operations in Philadelphia, and she has been working there for the past seven years, most recently as the COO. Ms. Martz will replace Rivard's current CEO, Timothy York, in June when he leaves the company for retirement.

> Subject: New CEO

> Dear Staff,
>
> Many of you knew in advance of the formal announcement that Katharine Martz will be the new CEO here at Rivard. We had a lot of positive feedback from the board, and we are pleased with their choice.
>
> Under Ms. Martz's leadership, Rivard will continue to be the nation's top provider of financial consulting services, and we expect further company growth. To formally welcome Ms. Martz to Rivard, there will be a reception at 10 A.M. on her first day of work. This will give you all the opportunity to meet her in person as well as hear more about her vision for Rivard. I hope to see you all there.
>
> Warmest regards,  
> Sylvia Cunningham

>
> Douglas

**Questions 191–195 refer to the following article, reservation confirmation, and e-mail.**

> ## Pearl Cruises Sets Sail
>
> Pearl Cruises, a luxury cruise company, started operating last month. Offering cruises going between Florida and the Caribbean, it is already receiving considerable praise from its passengers. The company was formed as a subsidiary of Comfort Tours with the intention of appealing to wealthier customers. While Comfort Tours offers many affordable vacation packages, Pearl Cruises creates the ultimate luxury vacation.
>
> CEO Brian Conner pointed out that the market for vacations and tourism is becoming more and more aimed for the upper class elite.
>
> "The average person is too busy with work and covering normal expenses these days," Mr. Conner said. "To make real profits, you have to go to where the money is."
>

There are three parts, and directions are given for each part. You are encouraged to answer as many questions as possible within the time allowed.

You must mark your answers on the separate answer sheet. Do not write your answers in your test book.

Following strong public support for improvements to environmental practices voiced at the Arlington City Council meeting in April, officials held another meeting on May 12 to find residents who might be interested in assisting with this goal by joining an advisory committee. ### Questions 158-160 refer to the following instructions.

**Casper Drain Cleaning Gel: Usage Instructions**

Casper Drain Cleaning Gel removes blockages from your kitchen or bathroom pipes. — [1] —. This product contains harsh chemicals, so it should be kept away from children at all times. If possible, make sure the room is well-ventilated prior to use.

1. Slowly pour 15 ounces of Casper Drain Cleaning Gel directly into the drain without diluting it. Please note that the gel will not harm the pipes, but it can cause damage to countertops and floor surfaces. — [2] —.
2. Let the gel sit in the drain for at least twenty minutes to allow it to work. If there is a major blockage, let it sit for at least forty-five minutes. — [3] —.
3. Flush the pipe with very hot, but not boiling water until the pipe is flowing well. — [4] —. Repeat Steps 1 and 2 if necessary.
4. Unused contents can be kept for at most a year, after which time, the ingredients may be less effective. Keep the bottle away from all heat sources while in storage.

### Questions 161-163 refer to the following letter.

Craig Nourse
216 Flint Street
Atlanta, GA 30303

Dear Mr. Nourse,

Because you are a member of the Chappell Bus Line Rewards Program, we would like to inform you about an update to our operational guidelines. We have adjusted the way that we deal with ticket cancellations for our bus trips. Previously, we did not charge for cancellations made before the travel day. From now on, customers will be charged 50% of the ticket price for cancellations made one week before the travel date or sooner, and 25% of the ticket price for cancellations made between one month and one week before the travel date. By implementing this policy, we can minimize our losses and maintain our current fares for all travelers. You may request a refund at one of our ticket counters. Please note that you can only be issued a refund by credit card or with Chappell Bus Line vouchers. This is because, as with all purchase transactions, these are the only forms of payment that we accept. For questions or comments, call our customer service center at 1-800-555-7663.

Sincerely,
The Chappell Bus Line Team

### Questions 164-167 refer to the following article.

**Food Lovers Turn Focus to South America**

LIMA (December 2) — Over the past year, the attention of shoppers and diners has turned toward foods produced in South America. The trend is spreading throughout the U.S. and Europe as people become more familiar with once-exotic foods such as quinoa, avocados, and acai berries. — [1] —.

Companies throughout South America, including here in Peru, are taking advantage of the growing popularity of South American goods. Food producers such as Arvelo Inc. and Massiani are boosting profits by signing agreements with restaurant chains rather than going through suppliers. — [2] —.

"More and more people are learning to cook with these products, and they also expect to see them as ingredients in restaurant dishes," reports Armando Bracho, the founder of ABComida, a large factory that processes food products. — [3] —. "The export of our goods contributes to a growing economy here at home."

Investing in this market makes sense now, but it was quite the risk at the time when Mr. Bracho did it over a decade ago. However, he's pleased with the results and with the skilled workforce he has found in Tarma. — [4] —. As a result, analysts predict that it will become a key player as more businesses attempt to add South American goods to their range.

"We are in the right place at the right time, and we're optimistic about the future," says Mr. Bracho.

### Questions 172-175 refer to the following information.

**Boating in Minnesota**

Minnesota is the "Land of 10,000 Lakes," so it offers many opportunities to enjoy the water. However, before you take your boat out, you must ensure that it is properly registered by the Parks and Wildlife Department. Please adhere to the following regulations:

1. Registration is required for all motorized boats and all boats that exceed 12 feet in length before they are used in public lakes. This should be done at the county office in which the titleholder resides, regardless of where the boat is being stored.
2. If you are transferring the possession of a new or used boat, either through sale or by gift, the new registration must be updated within 45 days. Failure to do so may result in a fine imposed on both parties.
3. The registration cannot be transferred to another boat. It is valid for a period of twelve months. The fee charged for registration varies from $25 to $60, depending on the length of the boat. A complete list of fees is available on the Parks and Wildlife Department's Web site.
4. Visitors from out of state do not have to register their boats in Minnesota if they are already registered in the home state. They can use public waters in Minnesota for up to 60 consecutive days without registration. After that time, a guest permit will be needed. Like the standard registration, it can be obtained from any county office.

5. Who is the intended audience of this information?
   (A) Boat owners
   (B) Boat tour participants
   (C) Boat manufacturers
   (D) Boat race competitors

6. What could happen to people who do not follow the registration procedures?
   (A) They may be disqualified from a contest.
   (B) They may be penalized financially.
   (C) They may lose the use of their boat.
   (D) They may have to undergo training.

7. What is NOT mentioned about boat registration?
   (A) It includes repair fees.
   (B) It lasts for one year.
   (C) Its cost depends on the boat's size.
   (D) It cannot be reassigned to another boat.

8. What is true about out-of-state visitors?
   (A) They are not allowed to bring their boats to Minnesota.
   (B) They can request a 60-day temporary registration.
   (C) They should visit a Web site to obtain a permit.
   (D) They do not need to register for the first two months.

### Questions 176-180 refer to the following article and letter.

**Vista Heights Property to be Transformed**

SUMMITVILLE (January 13)—Work has begun on a city property in the southern neighborhood of Vista Heights. Following years of planning, the groundbreaking began yesterday on a 20,000-seat stadium that will host baseball games, football tournaments, concerts, and more. Because of the population growth in Summitville over the past decade, the city is now large enough to support a sports venue of this size. The project will cost an estimated $1.8 million and is expected to yield tens of thousands of dollars in tax revenue for the city annually and give a boost to local businesses.

The stadium's main entrance will be on Maxwell Street and will include bronze statues of sports legends in an outdoor sculpture garden. The west entrance, on Everette Street, will be the site of an underground parking lot. The decision to put the parking lot underground was made because the historic Knowlton Manor is opposite the stadium on that street, and planners did not want to obstruct the view. City council members have also suggested changing Woodall Street, which runs along the northern end of the stadium, to pedestrian-only access. However, this proposal has not been finalized yet.

Dear Summitville City Council Members:

I am writing to you regarding the new project in the Vista Heights neighborhood. I operate a jewelry store that is right across the street from the stadium's site, on Everette Street. While I will appreciate the additional foot traffic that the stadium will bring, I also have some concerns about safety. Everette Street is a very busy roadway, and visitors to the stadium may be unfamiliar with the area, trying to cross the road without using the designated crosswalks. Would it be possible to change the speed limit on this street from 35 miles per hour to 20? I think this may be the easiest way to avoid potential injuries.

Thank you for your consideration,

Sincerely,

William Escobar
Owner, Sparkle Jewelry

176. What is the article mainly about?
     (A) The start of a sports tournament
     (B) The opening of a new store
     (C) The purchase of some city property
     (D) The construction of a new building

     (C) It will generate city funds.
     (D) It will encourage the use of public transportation.

     (B) Banning vehicles from the street
     (C) Using it as the main entrance
     (D) Making the road wider

     (C) It depends solely on foot traffic.
     (D) It is near a historic building.

To: All Delgado Insurance Employees  
From: Stacey Frederick, Office Supervisor  
Date: October 1  
Subject: Company car schedule

Starting from today, the details about who is using which company car will be posted on the staff section of our Web site. This will help us to track the usage more accurately. After filing the correct paperwork, you will not see the request posted until it has been confirmed by a manager. The list will be updated regularly by the head of administration.

We plan to use Sedan-1 and Sedan-2 exclusively for long-distance trips. We hope this will leave Sedan-3 and Sedan-4 available for last-minute usage, especially picking up clients from the airport. When making a request, it is necessary to list only staff members who will be driving the car. For example, Marta Bluhm and Brandy Rowe have tentative plans to travel to New York together next month, and both of them will be driving. However, Heather Kirk does not need to list her name for the trip with Jason Blake to Boston, as she will be only a passenger.

Please contact me with any questions.

Sincerely,  
Stacey

- Please inform Alex Holt if your trip gets canceled or postponed so that he can update the schedule.
- This schedule is for car use only. If you need train or airline tickets booked, please speak to Michelle Hollenbeck. Information about these trips will not be posted on the company Web site.

| November 1–5   | Jason Blake        | Richmond, VA   | Sedan-1      |
| November 5–7   | Sheena Duran       | Pittsburgh, PA | Sedan-2      |

Get the freshest produce available at the Sacramento Farmers Market!  
Every Wednesday and Saturday, Graham Park (near the outdoor stage)  
Site opens at 6 A.M. for vendor setup and 7 A.M. for customers.

The Sacramento Farmers Market has been a staple in the agricultural community for over a decade. Many chefs from local restaurants get their ingredients here, and members of the public are welcome as well. There is no admission charge for shoppers.

You can shop by category to find exactly what you're looking for.  
Northwest Section: Vegetables, Northeast Section: Fruits, Southwest Section: Flowers, Southeast Section: Nuts and Seeds

**Notice to Sacramento Farmers Market Vendors**

June 3

Effective immediately, we no longer accept reservations for booths. These will be assigned on a first-come, first-served basis on the market days, and vendors should select their spots from those available when they arrive.

As usual, there is a $15 charge for renting the booth area for the day. Those who use a punch card can receive a reduced price, as you'll get to attend ten events for $100, making booth rental $10 per visit. Please note that you must either present your card to be punched or pay the $15 cash fee. In addition, we are still offering banner printing for those who want a large sign for their booth. You must request it at least 3 days in advance, and the cost depends on the size and the number of colors used.

For more information, contact Lorraine Newton at l.newton@calvinco.net.

### Questions 196-200 refer to the following e-mail, job posting, and memo.

To: Ronald Vogler <voglerr@raintreepublishing.com>  
From: Kiyoko Izumi <izumik@raintreepublishing.com>  
Date: March 21  
Subject: Job posting

Dear Ronald,

As you know, we would like to begin advertising for the junior editor position sometime this week. Please prepare a job posting and send it to the major job Web sites no later than Friday. We need it to be well-written in order to draw the attention of prospective applicants. The posting should include information about the necessary qualifications, the salary and time off, and our in-house testing process. You should also list the interview dates so that people can plan in advance.

Thank you for taking care of this matter!

Kiyoko

**Raintree Publishing Seeks Junior Editor**

Raintree Publishing—a publisher of several prominent scientific journals, including Biology Quarterly and The Journal of Clinical Research—is seeking a full-time junior editor for our publications. You must have at least 2 years of editing experience and a bachelor's or master's degree in a scientific field. You must be detail-oriented with strong organizational skills and the ability to meet tight deadlines. Please note that those who pass the screening phase will be asked to visit our site in person for a 30-minute computer-based test followed by a brief writing test.

The compensation package includes an annual starting salary of $40,000 with opportunities for promotion within the organization. The benefits package also includes fifteen paid days off per year and medical insurance.

The work will begin on May 17, and you will train with the senior editor the first day and the current junior editor for the remainder of the week. Applications can be sent to Ronald Vogler at voglerr@raintreepublishing.com. To get a better picture of the tasks you would perform on a daily basis, check out our Behind the Scenes blog, accessible from our homepage.

To: All Raintree Publishing Staff  
From: Christine Fowler, Office Manager  
Date: May 20  
Subject: Re: Farewells

Senior Editor Esther Robey has suggested holding a reception tomorrow for two of our employees who will be leaving Raintree Publishing. Marketing Associate Amanda O'Conner will transfer to our office in Toronto next week, taking on a promotion to Marketing Director for our Canadian operations. Junior Editor Marcus Payton will be leaving our company for a position with the San Diego Times. He has been training his replacement this week. We are thankful for the hard work of both of these individuals. The farewell reception will be held in Conference Room A at 4 P.M. I have prepared a card for each of them, so please stop by my office sometime today to sign them. Thank you!

### Questions 161-163 refer to the following article.

**Exciting News for Outdoor Enthusiasts**

WHITESTONE (March 22)—A proposal to deal with the neglected pond at the northern end of Amato Park has been finalized, city officials confirmed this morning. A wooden walkway will be added around the pond, and signs to teach people about wildlife will be posted along the route.

Whitestone residents were overwhelmingly in favor of the news, despite the high cost of the project. "It's difficult for people to enjoy the pond in its current state," said Maria Sheley, who lives near the park. "By adding a walkway, everyone will be able to get great views of the pond without getting muddy or disturbing the vegetation growing near the water. It's worth every penny."

The work will not only help visitors to the park but animals as well. As part of the project, researchers will work toward reversing the effect that pollution has had on the pond. They will introduce plants that act as natural filters to help keep the water clear. "Before and After" pictures of the project will be posted on the city's Web site. For more information, contact the Parks and Recreation Department.

     (A) To announce the construction of a new park

     (C) To explain plans to improve a body of water
     (D) To report on a pond's safety record

     (C) She wishes that more people would get involved.
     (D) She believes that the deadline for the work is reasonable.

     (B) distinct

### Questions 168-171 refer to the following article.

SOUTHDALE (November 18)—The Southdale Tigers baseball team announced at a press conference yesterday that the Southdale Stadium will be known as Meridian Stadium from January 1. The naming rights for the stadium were sold to food industry giant Meridian Foods. Meridian executives hope the move will help to not only support local sports but also promote its efforts to branch out into the energy drink market, as a new line of these goods hit supermarkets just a few weeks ago.

The money raised from the naming rights will be directed to a number of areas. Some of the funds will be used to increase the salaries of the players, and the team's owners will be given a share of these funds. The team will also purchase a new bus that will be used for trips shorter than 200 miles. However, ongoing projects—such as fixing worn seats and purchasing equipment upgrades for the concession stands—will still be funded by revenue from tickets only.

Elsie Blanchard, who oversaw the legal aspects of the deal on behalf of the team, said she was pleased with the outcome of the negotiations and that the contract for naming rights will be up for renewal after five years.

### Questions 172-175 refer to the following article.

**Giving Back to the Community**
by Adelaide Devries

ROSEDALE (May 3)—The Rosedale Community Club (RCC) is planning an extensive cleanup project at Belhaven Beach and is looking for support from the community. — [1] —. Unlike other cleanup projects undertaken by the club in recent years, this one is also aimed at raising money to support a local literature campaign. The club's members will be picking up litter at the beach all day on Saturday, May 18. — [2] —. The RCC is asking for donations from local businesses for each pound collected.

"Belhaven Beach is one of the few places in the area where visitors can feel like they are a part of nature. — [3] —. We wanted to do something to change that," said RCC President Billie Wein. "To help our cause, businesses can donate as little as five cents per pound of collected trash. Of course, we welcome much more than that. Each business can decide for themselves what amount would be suitable for their own situation."

All money raised by the project will go toward the purchase of books, with a goal of purchasing at least five hundred of them. — [4] —. These will be distributed to students at Rosedale Elementary School. The books will be theirs to keep, and RCC members hope that their efforts will encourage children to read more over the summer break.

For more information, please contact Billie Wein at b.wein@rosedalecc.org.

Dear Ms. Cabrera,

As you order items from Bedell Cosmetics regularly, we would like to ask you to join our new Consumer Review Scheme. Through this program, you would receive free samples from Bedell Cosmetics. Cora Nielsen

**Product Information:** Tamarac Hand Cream, available in 50ml and 70ml

**Reviewer Information:** Madeline Cabrera, 15 previous reviews posted

I highly recommend Tamarac Hand Cream for a number of reasons. First of all, it smells great, and the silky texture left my hands feeling soft without the greasiness you often get with other products. I saw an improvement in my skin's appearance right away, with dry flakes disappearing. After repeated use, this effect was magnified. Considering the quality of this product, the retail price is fair, and it is a nice complement to other Bedell products. If you have sensitive skin, you should put just a dab of the cream on a small patch of skin first to see if you have a reaction. This is a good idea even if you are not allergic to any of the ingredients on the package.

**Generator Rental for Commercial, Governmental, and Residential Needs**

Renting a generator can save you money, as you can rent the device for a short period of time. All Burwell Generators are inspected by our technicians prior to delivery, ensuring that you'll have the power you need exactly when you need it. We use designs that meet all industry regulations and have built-in safety features. In emergency situations, generators can be provided in less than an hour. For pre-booked rentals, we ask that customers provide the following information:

1. The power output needed (listed in watts)
2. The user's previous experience with generators
3. The number of days the generator is needed
4. The type of place where the generator will be used (Please note that most campsites ban the use of these devices on site due to the noise disturbances they may cause.)

For more information, call our helpline at 555-9467 during business hours (M–F, 9 A.M.–6 P.M.). For emergency bookings, call 555-9400.

**MEMO**

**From:** Marie Higgins  
**To:** All Employees  
**Date:** Friday, 18 September  
**Subject:** Next Week

A recent safety inspection of our building found several faulty electric outlets. Electricians will be coming in this coming Monday, 21 September to repair them. For safety reasons, no one else is allowed in the building during that time, so we will not be open. If you had scheduled a client visit, please apologize for the inconvenience and change your appointment time or location.

Members of the IT Department will not be expected to do any work on that day since they will be unable to access our hardware. We will return to regular operations on the following day.

Thank you for your understanding and cooperation.

The event will be held on Saturday, October 17, at 8 P.M. at Trevisan Plaza. A reception table will be set up at the double doors on Lexington Avenue. [2] —.

Drinks will be served. Although only your presence is expected, we understand that some people would like to bring flowers or gifts. [3] —. Instead of this, please donate to support the History Guard's general operating fund. [4] —.

### Questions 168-171 refer to the following article.

**Pizzeria Delivering Up Change**  
by Ashton Kahl

SPRINGFIELD (September 4)—Pizza restaurant chain Tony's Pizzeria has announced plans for a revolutionary program for take-out orders. After seeing the success of selling reusable cups for coffee at his local coffee house, owner Tony Rossi got the idea to apply this practice to his restaurants. The Mega Box Program, the first of its kind among pizza restaurants, will give customers the option of purchasing a reusable aluminum pizza box for their take-out orders. Rossi hopes the program will help to drastically cut down on the number of cardboard pizza boxes that are thrown away, as these cannot be recycled due to their absorption of grease.

To participate in the program, customers can purchase boxes at any Tony's Pizzeria branch. When they use the boxes to take home pizzas, they can get fifty cents off their order for each box used. "We're giving more of a discount than we save from the unused box, but the important thing is to create opportunities for sustainable practices," Rossi said. Since the boxes are made out of aluminum, they retain heat well and are easy to recycle after they can no longer be used. The company's Web site features a page explaining the benefits of the program as well as information about how to wash and store the boxes to keep them in good condition. Rossi expects business to pick up once customers hear about the change, as consumers are becoming more active in supporting businesses with environmentally friendly practices.

### Questions 176-180 refer to the following article and Web page.

**More Than a Gallery**

While Kerrville is often referred to as the heart of the painting and sculpting community, with a high number of galleries compared to its small population, there has been a surprising lack of options for purchasing supplies in town. That will change when the Pearcy Gallery completes its expansion project. The new section of the building will house an art supply shop with a wide range of items.

The Pearcy Gallery will also have more space for its classes, which run several nights a week and are designed for different groups, such as children, teens, adults, and seniors, all at a variety of ability levels. There are also plans to add a monthly lecture series to give artists the opportunity to further their understanding of the business side of the art world, with practical tips on pricing their work, marketing, and building a customer base.

---

**Available Positions at the Pearcy Gallery**  
Positions: Shop Manager, Shop Clerks  
Updated: October 5

The Pearcy Gallery will open its retail shop on November 1, so we are currently seeking a full-time shop manager as well as several full- and part-time shop clerks. The new shop will feature painting supplies and other materials that can be used by artists young and old, and everyone in between. All employees receive a competitive compensation package, with paid vacation time for full-time employees. Teamwork among the staff is essential, so we will be looking for people who fit that mindset.

The shop manager must have at least 2 years of management experience. Duties include ordering and managing stock, dealing with customer complaints, making bank deposits, and maintaining financial records. Shop clerks will be responsible for assisting customers with finding items, carrying out transactions, and stocking shelves. During guest lectures, they will assist with setting up seating as well as selling tickets at the door.

To apply for a position at the Pearcy Gallery, please e-mail a résumé and cover letter to Curtis Nelson at c.nelson@pearcygallery.com no later than October 17. Interviews will be held the week of October 20.

- (D) They are concerned about the decreasing population.

**To:** All Lahey Enterprises Employees  
**From:** Rodney Chapman, Office Manager  
**Date:** March 6  
**Subject:** Please read

The owner of our office building allocates a certain number of secure storage lockers to each business in the building. Our company has access to twenty-five lockers in total, ten in the west wing lobby and fifteen in the east wing lobby. All lockers can be accessed around the clock. There is an annual fee of $80 for the use of the storage lockers, and this fee is taken directly out of the employee's paycheck. To comply with insurance requirements, the building's security team inspects the lockers once per quarter to make sure they are in good working condition and that they do not contain any prohibited items. Notice is given in advance of these inspections.

I am currently updating my master list of which locker is associated with which employee. This information must also be provided to the building owner. I have had confirmation from most of the current renters, but I'm still waiting to hear from those listed on the attached document. If you are on the list, please hand in the Locker Renewal/Cancellation form no later than March 25 to indicate whether or not you want to continue using the locker. Should there be cancellations in the usage of the lockers, the available ones will be re-assigned, starting with the names at the top of the waiting list.

**Current Locker Rentals**

- West wing lockers are designed solely for bicycle storage.
- East wing lockers have space for hanging clothing.

**High-quality work boots for your safety and comfort!**

What sets Fontaine Footwear apart?

**Subject:** Work boots

Dear Mr. Maloy,

**To:** All Gillman Construction Employees <stafflist@gillman-construction.com>  
**From:** Leo Riggins <leoriggins@gillman-construction.com>  
**Date:** April 13  
**Subject:** Work boots

Following a number of injuries from stepping on nails and other sharp objects, Gillman Construction will change the rules for employee footwear. Starting from April 23, all employees must wear steel-soled boots while on duty. We have had too many problems with those using rubber. Having steel in the toe is still optional, but we highly recommend it. This change is expected to improve the safety of our employees.

Previously, we required employees to purchase their own footwear. However, due to this change, our company will make the purchase of the first pair of boots for each of our 37 employees. We plan to make the purchase from Fontaine Footwear on April 17, so please inform Tracey O'Donnell in the HR department of your preferred size before then. The company offers men's sizes from 7-15 and women's sizes from 5-11. Both the men's and women's boots have half sizes available. Questions about this or any other safety matters should be directed to your immediate supervisor.

On Friday, May 17, and Saturday, May 18, free health screenings will be available to all Lexington residents.

The screenings will take place from 10 A.M. to 7 P.M. at the Lexington Community Center. The free service will include a comprehensive health risk assessment by medical professionals. Tests include blood pressure, cholesterol level, vision, a chest X-ray, and more.

"Everyone is welcome, but those who cannot afford medical insurance are especially encouraged to attend," said Lucy Chen.

### Questions 161–164 refer to the following e-mail.

To: Candice Seward <c.seward@litchfieldco.com>
From: Guillermo Medina <g.medina@litchfieldco.com>
Date: October 29
Subject: Banquet

Dear Candice,

I have found three caterers that may be suitable for our year-end client appreciation banquet. Last year, we had around 150 participants from 30 different companies, and we're expecting about the same this year. I've spoken to Amanda Delgado of Silver Spoon, Paul Mundo of Cuisine Co., and Troy Irving of Troy's Catering. All have confirmed that they are able to handle a group of our size on the event date at Ventura Hall here in Portland.

We used Ms. Delgado's company last year, but there were some complaints about the service. That's why I'm leaning toward selecting one of the other two. Mr. Mundo's business has excellent reviews, but since his team would have to travel from out of town, there would be an additional transportation charge. I've had Mr. Irving's food at other events, and it was delicious every time. His prices are on the higher side, though, which might be an issue, as we're still looking for ways to cut costs for the event.

Let's get together sometime this week to make a decision about which business to hire. The rest of the plans are moving along well. Ted Cassady has finished designing the invitations, and they look fantastic. I'll get them printed and send one to each company next week.

Cheers!

Guillermo

### Questions 168–171 refer to the following e-mail.

To: <editor@sciencenewsmag.net>
From: <k.moreno@nicolapharma.com>
Date: February 5
Subject: Nicola Pharmaceuticals

Dear Science News Magazine Editor,

I am the lead researcher at Nicola Pharmaceuticals, and our recent breakthrough in treating chronic fatigue was featured in an article by Barbara Levy. — [1] —. Although I am thankful for the exposure of this important advancement, I was disappointed to see that my team of researchers, known as Research Team B5, were the only people mentioned in regard to the project, as we carried out the analysis. — [2] —. We were just a part of the team that worked hard to make this new treatment a reality.

Members of Research Team B6 were not mentioned at all, despite their valuable contributions. They recruited patients for each stage of the trial. It is a shame that their names were omitted, and I'm also very surprised that this happened. — [3] —. Ms. Levy e-mailed me a set of questions in preparation for the article, which I answered completely. My first e-mail response explained the details of my own team's work, and my second e-mail outlined the work of the B6 team. It seems the information from the second e-mail was not addressed in her writing at all. I ask that you print a brief article to correct this oversight. — [4] —. The sooner your readers are given the full picture, the better.

Thank you for your consideration.

Sincerely,

Kevin Moreno

### Questions 176–180 refer to the following e-mails.

To: Kenneth Allard <kallard@corporateelite.com>
From: Cynthia Holt <cynthia@dawsonbaycruises.com>
Date: July 30
Subject: Proposed Contract
Attachment: corporate_elite_contract.docx

Dear Mr. Allard,

Thank you for contacting Dawson Bay Cruises about tours of Dawson Bay for participants in your professional development workshops. Our fleet has been in operation for ten years, and we have an unblemished safety record. Each boat ride is hosted by a guide who can speak both English and Spanish, and we offer cold drinks—such as bottled water, juice, and soft drinks—on board. We operate from Pier 1, which is the closest one from the metered parking area, so it's easy to find us.

I've attached a proposed contract for this service. Since you'll need tours regularly, even during our low season, we are able to offer you a heavily discounted per-person rate. The proposal assumes that you will bring us at least two groups per month, on the last day of their workshop to give them a break. For each tour, one of your staff members should provide us with a printed list of everyone who will board the boat. This is required by the Water Safety Association (WSA) and can just be submitted upon arrival. It does not need to be sent in advance.

I look forward to hearing from you!

Cynthia Holt

> **Drop-off Location:** Amaro Street branch, Atlanta, GA
>
> For urgent questions or inquiries, please call 310-555-3449. Non-urgent inquiries and formal complaints can be sent to our main branch at 1325 Wilson Avenue, Los Angeles, CA 90014. Customers can make cancellations at no charge up to 48 hours before the scheduled pick-up.
>
> Please note that there are only two seats available for passengers. As seatbelts are required for all passengers, our insurance company does not allow passengers to ride in the back of the van. If you plan on transporting more than two passengers, you will need a separate vehicle.

---

> **Vernon Moving Vans: Roadside Assistance**
>
> Round-the-clock roadside assistance is available for all vans rented from Vernon Moving Vans. Should you experience an issue with your vehicle, call us toll-free at 1-800-555-4433. We will dispatch a mechanic to attempt to make repairs. If he or she is unsuccessful after thirty minutes, a replacement vehicle will be supplied to you, and our staff will help you to move your items to the new vehicle. For minor repairs that you are able to handle yourself, such as replacing a flat tire, please call us first to inform us of the change. Then we can have our mechanics double-check the work carefully when the vehicle is returned to one of our branches.

### Questions 191–195 refer to the following job posting, letter, and e-mail.

> **Now Hiring: Office Assistant**
> **Edgewood Dental Clinic (EDC)**
>
> Edgewood Dental Clinic (EDC) has been seeing and treating patients in Fresno for a full decade, and is looking to take on a new full-time staff member. Although we have enough dentists and nurses, we are in need of someone for other office duties such as answering phones, greeting incoming patients, and inputting patient records into our local database. Experience in an office setting is not required, although familiarity with general office software is a must. If you are interested in the position, send a copy of your résumé by mail to Ms. Amanda Carr, 658 Edgewood Avenue, Fresno, CA 93721 or by e-mail to a.carr@edc.com by 15 June. Ms. Carr will conduct preliminary interviews, but our head doctor will conduct the final interview after returning from the Dentistry Awards Dinner in New York later this month.

---

13 June
Ms. Amanda Carr
658 Edgewood Avenue
Fresno, CA 94721

Dear Ms. Carr,

This letter is in response to your job posting for an office assistant. My résumé is enclosed.

As you can see, I graduated from San Jose University two years ago. Although I do not have official experience doing office work, I am certified in several programs including DocuFile and DigiMemo.

Given the chance, I am sure that my diligent work ethic and friendly personality will contribute to making me an important member of your staff. Thank you in advance for your consideration. I hope to hear from you soon.

Sincerely,

Christopher Shields
Christopher Shields

Subject: Office assistant position

Dear Mr. Shields,

Thank you for coming in for a follow-up interview with Dr. William Crosby. He was impressed by your confidence, knowledge, and eagerness to learn. We would like to offer you the office assistant position.

You mentioned that you are available to start immediately. Please give us some time to prepare some materials to train you properly. It would be best if you could report to our office on the morning of 12 July for general orientation. You will also have to fill out some basic paperwork with our HR department, which shouldn't take any more than one hour. Your first day of work will be the next day, Tuesday, 13 July.

Thank you again, and we look forward to working with you.

Sincerely,

Amanda Carr
HR Department Head, EDC

### Questions 196–200 refer to the following notice, e-mail, and map.

> **NOTICE TO GREENWOOD LIBRARY PATRONS**
>
> Last month, the Greenwood Library received a donation of an extensive collection of rare stamps from a local supporter's estate. After considering the library's financial needs and its space limitations, the Greenwood Library Advisory Committee (GLAC) has determined that the best option is to sell some of the stamps to raise funds for the library. An auction will be held at the library on Saturday, February 4, in Lecture Room 1. Very rare and valuable stamps will be sold individually, and those that are somewhat rare will be sold in groups of 6–10 stamps. The Greenwood Museum of History will accept the leftover stamps and make a permanent display for them to replace its existing Pioneer Life exhibit. We're seeking volunteers to assist with the auction. We'll need people to pass out flyers around town on Saturday, January 28. We'll also need people to set up tables and displays on February 3 and to keep records of financial donations on February 4. For more information, e-mail Collette Robbins at c.robbins@greenwoodlib.org.

---

To: Renato Martinez <martinezr31@calhounmail.com>
From: Collette Robbins <c.robbins@greenwoodlib.org>
Date: January 16
Subject: Stamp auction

Dear Mr. Martinez,

Thank you for your interest in our upcoming stamp auction. Regarding your question, we will only be selling stamps that are in mint or near-mint condition, so you can be assured of the quality. The stamps had been mounted in special stockpages, which means that they were held in place by a clear plastic pocket rather than being stuck to the page. You can see photos of the stamps that will be up for auction on the library's Web site. I love your idea of adding these stamps to your mixed media artwork, so I hope you find some that will work well for your project.

I'd also like to thank you for offering to be a volunteer. I'll see you in person when you report for volunteer duty at the library on January 28. Please arrive by 10 A.M.

Sincerely,

Collette Robbins
